Review the complete diff above. This contains all code changes in the PR.


OBJECTIVE:
Perform a security-focused code review to identify HIGH-CONFIDENCE security vulnerabilities that could have real exploitation potential. This is not a general code review - focus ONLY on security implications newly added by this PR. Do not comment on existing security concerns.

CRITICAL INSTRUCTIONS:
1. MINIMIZE FALSE POSITIVES: Only flag issues where you're >80% confident of actual exploitability
2. AVOID NOISE: Skip theoretical issues, style concerns, or low-impact findings
3. FOCUS ON IMPACT: Prioritize vulnerabilities that could lead to unauthorized access, data breaches, or system compromise
4. EXCLUSIONS: Do NOT report the following issue types:
   - Denial of Service (DOS) vulnerabilities, even if they allow service disruption
   - Secrets or sensitive data stored on disk (these are handled by other processes)
   - Rate limiting or resource exhaustion issues

SECURITY CATEGORIES TO EXAMINE:

**Input Validation Vulnerabilities:**
- SQL injection via unsanitized user input
- Command injection in system calls or subprocesses
- XXE injection in XML parsing
- Template injection in templating engines
- NoSQL injection in database queries
- Path traversal in file operations

**Authentication & Authorization Issues:**
- Authentication bypass logic
- Privilege escalation paths
- Session management flaws
- JWT token vulnerabilities
- Authorization logic bypasses

**Crypto & Secrets Management:**
- Hardcoded API keys, passwords, or tokens
- Weak cryptographic algorithms or implementations
- Improper key storage or management
- Cryptographic randomness issues
- Certificate validation bypasses

**Injection & Code Execution:**
- Remote code execution via deseralization
- Pickle injection in Python
- YAML deserialization vulnerabilities
- Eval injection in dynamic code execution
- XSS vulnerabilities in web applications (reflected, stored, DOM-based)

**Data Exposure:**
- Sensitive data logging or storage
- PII handling violations
- API endpoint data leakage
- Debug information exposure

Additional notes:
- Even if something is only exploitable from the local network, it can still be a HIGH severity issue

ANALYSIS METHODOLOGY:

Phase 1 - Repository Context Research (Use file search tools):
- Identify existing security frameworks and libraries in use
- Look for established secure coding patterns in the codebase
- Examine existing sanitization and validation patterns
- Understand the project's security model and threat model

Phase 2 - Comparative Analysis:
- Compare new code changes against existing security patterns
- Identify deviations from established secure practices
- Look for inconsistent security implementations
- Flag code that introduces new attack surfaces

Phase 3 - Vulnerability Assessment:
- Examine each modified file for security implications
- Trace data flow from user inputs to sensitive operations
- Look for privilege boundaries being crossed unsafely
- Identify injection points and unsafe deserialization

REQUIRED OUTPUT FORMAT:

You MUST output your findings in markdown. The markdown output should contain the file, line number, severity, category (e.g. `sql_injection` or `xss`), description, exploit scenario, and fix recommendation.

For example:

# Vuln 1: XSS: `foo.py:42`

* Severity: High
* Description: User input from `username` parameter is directly interpolated into HTML without escaping, allowing reflected XSS attacks
* Exploit Scenario: Attacker crafts URL like /bar?q=<script>alert(document.cookie)</script> to execute JavaScript in victim's browser, enabling session hijacking or data theft
* Recommendation: Use Flask's escape() function or Jinja2 templates with auto-escaping enabled for all user inputs rendered in HTML

SEVERITY GUIDELINES:
- **HIGH**: Directly exploitable vulnerabilities leading to RCE, data breach, or authentication bypass
- **MEDIUM**: Vulnerabilities requiring specific conditions but with significant impact
- **LOW**: Defense-in-depth issues or lower-impact vulnerabilities

CONFIDENCE SCORING:
- 0.9-1.0: Certain exploit path identified, tested if possible
- 0.8-0.9: Clear vulnerability pattern with known exploitation methods
- 0.7-0.8: Suspicious pattern requiring specific conditions to exploit
- Below 0.7: Don't report (too speculative)

FINAL REMINDER:
Focus on HIGH and MEDIUM findings only. Better to miss some theoretical issues than flood the report with false positives. Each finding should be something a security engineer would confidently raise in a PR review.

FALSE POSITIVE FILTERING:

> You do not need to run commands to reproduce the vulnerability, just read the code to determine if it is a real vulnerability. Do not use the bash tool or write to any files.
>
> HARD EXCLUSIONS - Automatically exclude findings matching these patterns:
> 1. Denial of Service (DOS) vulnerabilities or resource exhaustion attacks.
> 2. Secrets or credentials stored on disk if they are otherwise secured.
> 3. Rate limiting concerns or service overload scenarios.
> 4. Memory consumption or CPU exhaustion issues.
> 5. Lack of input validation on non-security-critical fields without proven security impact.
> 6. Input sanitization concerns for GitHub Action workflows unless they are clearly triggerable via untrusted input.
> 7. A lack of hardening measures. Code is not expected to implement all security best practices, only flag concrete vulnerabilities.
> 8. Race conditions or timing attacks that are theoretical rather than practical issues. Only report a race condition if it is concretely problematic.
> 9. Vulnerabilities related to outdated third-party libraries. These are managed separately and should not be reported here.
> 10. Memory safety issues such as buffer overflows or use-after-free-vulnerabilities are impossible in rust. Do not report memory safety issues in rust or any other memory safe languages.
> 11. Files that are only unit tests or only used as part of running tests.
> 12. Log spoofing concerns. Outputting un-sanitized user input to logs is not a vulnerability.
> 13. SSRF vulnerabilities that only control the path. SSRF is only a concern if it can control the host or protocol.
> 14. Including user-controlled content in AI system prompts is not a vulnerability.
> 15. Regex injection. Injecting untrusted content into a regex is not a vulnerability.
> 16. Regex DOS concerns.
> 16. Insecure documentation. Do not report any findings in documentation files such as markdown files.
> 17. A lack of audit logs is not a vulnerability.
>
> PRECEDENTS -
> 1. Logging high value secrets in plaintext is a vulnerability. Logging URLs is assumed to be safe.
> 2. UUIDs can be assumed to be unguessable and do not need to be validated.
> 3. Environment variables and CLI flags are trusted values. Attackers are generally not able to modify them in a secure environment. Any attack that relies on controlling an environment variable is invalid.
> 4. Resource management issues such as memory or file descriptor leaks are not valid.
> 5. Subtle or low impact web vulnerabilities such as tabnabbing, XS-Leaks, prototype pollution, and open redirects should not be reported unless they are extremely high confidence.
> 6. React and Angular are generally secure against XSS. These frameworks do not need to sanitize or escape user input unless it is using dangerouslySetInnerHTML, bypassSecurityTrustHtml, or similar methods. Do not report XSS vulnerabilities in React or Angular components or tsx files unless they are using unsafe methods.
> 7. Most vulnerabilities in github action workflows are not exploitable in practice. Before validating a github action workflow vulnerability ensure it is concrete and has a very specific attack path.
> 8. A lack of permission checking or authentication in client-side JS/TS code is not a vulnerability. Client-side code is not trusted and does not need to implement these checks, they are handled on the server-side. The same applies to all flows that send untrusted data to the backend, the backend is responsible for validating and sanitizing all inputs.
> 9. Only include MEDIUM findings if they are obvious and concrete issues.
> 10. Most vulnerabilities in ipython notebooks (*.ipynb files) are not exploitable in practice. Before validating a notebook vulnerability ensure it is concrete and has a very specific attack path where untrusted input can trigger the vulnerability.
> 11. Logging non-PII data is not a vulnerability even if the data may be sensitive. Only report logging vulnerabilities if they expose sensitive information such as secrets, passwords, or personally identifiable information (PII).
> 12. Command injection vulnerabilities in shell scripts are generally not exploitable in practice since shell scripts generally do not run with untrusted user input. Only report command injection vulnerabilities in shell scripts if they are concrete and have a very specific attack path for untrusted input.
>
> SIGNAL QUALITY CRITERIA - For remaining findings, assess:
> 1. Is there a concrete, exploitable vulnerability with a clear attack path?
> 2. Does this represent a real security risk vs theoretical best practice?
> 3. Are there specific code locations and reproduction steps?
> 4. Would this finding be actionable for a security team?
>
> For each finding, assign a confidence score from 1-10:
> - 1-3: Low confidence, likely false positive or noise
> - 4-6: Medium confidence, needs investigation
> - 7-10: High confidence, likely true vulnerability

START ANALYSIS:

Begin your analysis now. Do this in 3 steps:

1. Use a sub-task to identify vulnerabilities. Use the repository exploration tools to understand the codebase context, then analyze the PR changes for security implications. In the prompt for this sub-task, include all of the above.
2. Then for each vulnerability identified by the above sub-task, create a new sub-task to filter out false-positives. Launch these sub-tasks as parallel sub-tasks. In the prompt for these sub-tasks, include everything in the "FALSE POSITIVE FILTERING" instructions.
3. Filter out any vulnerabilities where the sub-task reported a confidence less than 8.

Your final reply must contain the markdown report and nothing else.

The callees re-check tenant *ownership* only; they have no notion of HITL approval, so owning the bound resource is sufficient to make the dispatch fire.\n\n**Why callees do not mitigate:** `campaign-run`, `nurture-dispatch`, and `orchestrate-content` validate only that the caller owns the campaign/enrollment/account (`.eq("user_id", user.id)`) and self-bill. None of them re-evaluate the intent-plan HITL state. They are designed to be the *outward action*; the only gate protecting them from running un-reviewed is the (bypassed) `status===\'approved\'` check in `intent-execute`. So the outward action executes for real.\n\n**Fix recommendation (any one; first is the established pattern):**\n- **Extend the guard to INSERT.** Re-bind the transition guard as `BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE` and, in the INSERT branch, block any non-`service_role` caller from inserting a row whose `status` is not in the allowed *initial* set (`\'proposed\'`). Mirror the role check already used (`auth.role() IS DISTINCT FROM \'service_role\'`). This matches the codebase pattern in `20260602150000` where the sibling tenant guards explicitly cover both INSERT and UPDATE branches. Concretely, in `guard_intent_plan_transition()` handle `TG_OP=\'INSERT\'`: `IF NEW.status NOT IN (\'proposed\',\'auto_approved\',\'pending_hitl\') AND auth.role() IS DISTINCT FROM \'service_role\' THEN RAISE EXCEPTION ... USING ERRCODE=\'42501\'; END IF;` (note: even `auto_approved` is AI-set; the safest set is `\'proposed\'` only for client inserts, since `intent-orchestrate` runs as service_role and is exempt). Then `CREATE TRIGGER ... BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE`.\n- **OR** tighten RLS: replace `intent_plans_insert_own` with `WITH CHECK (auth.uid() = user_id AND status IN (\'proposed\'))` (or whatever the legitimate client-insert states are), so a client can never plant `approved`/`executed`. Since `intent-orchestrate` inserts via service_role (bypasses RLS), this does not break the legitimate creation path.\n- **OR** make `intent-execute` not trust a directly-planted approval: require an auditable approval provenance (e.g., an `approved_by`/`approved_at` set only by the service-role approve branch, and check it is non-null and consistent in the execute path) rather than trusting the `status` column alone. The migration comment even notes the INSERT vector is the threat ("AI mislabel OR direct PostgREST tampering"), yet only the UPDATE vector is closed.\n\n---\n\n### Items examined and deliberately NOT flagged\n\n- **JWT forwarding (`intent-execute/index.ts:125`).** The forwarded `Authorization: authHeader` is the *caller\'s own* user JWT (read from `req.headers.get("Authorization")` at line 57 and validated via `getUser()` at line 60), **not** `serviceKey`. The service key is only used for the local `admin` client. No service-key leakage to sub-functions. Safe.\n- **SSRF in `fetch(`${url}/functions/v1/${target}`)` (line 123).** `url` is `Deno.env.get("SUPABASE_URL")` (trusted env) and `target` is one of three hard-coded string literals (`"campaign-run"`, `"nurture-dispatch"`, `"orchestrate-content"`) — never user-controlled. No SSRF.\n- **Cross-tenant dispatch via bindings.** `validate_intent_plan_bindings()` enforces `campaign.user_id = plan.user_id` / `enrollment.user_id = plan.user_id` on INSERT/UPDATE, and the callees independently re-check ownership against the JWT subject (`.eq("user_id", user.id)`), returning 404 for a foreign id. A tenant cannot make the dispatch act on another tenant\'s campaign/enrollment. (The binding trigger *does* correctly fire on INSERT, unlike the transition guard.) Not exploitable cross-tenant.\n- **`nurture-dispatch` consent gate / `delivery:\'gated\'`.** External delivery is still gated (`metadata.delivery=\'gated\'`) and consent-gated. The enrollment-binding path of the HITL bypass therefore causes a charge + recorded `message_sent` event but not an actual external send. Still in scope of Finding 1 as an un-reviewed outward action + mcoCoins spend, but no independent new finding.\n- **The `auth.role()=\'service_role\'` role detection** in the new triggers matches the established, reviewed codebase pattern (`20260602150000`, `20260603220000`) and uses NULL-safe `IS DISTINCT FROM`. Correct as written; the defect is the missing INSERT binding, not the role check.\n\n**Net:** one HIGH finding. The PR\'s stated security objective — "a tenant cannot self-approve and ride into a dispatch" — is not met, because the guarding trigger covers UPDATE but the privilege escalation is reachable through a direct INSERT with `status=\'approved\'`, which the executor\'s `status===\'approved\'` re-validation then honors as if it were a human approval.'}, {'type': 'text', 'text': "agentId: ab7e79a55b13361f3 (use SendMessage with to: 'ab7e79a55b13361f3' to continue this agent)\n<usage>subagent_tokens: 85727\ntool_uses: 14\nduration_ms: 88753</usage>"}]

The identification sub-task surfaced **one HIGH finding — and it's real and serious.** My transition guard is `BEFORE UPDATE` only, but the original `intent_plans` RLS `insert_own` policy has no status restriction and the CHECK allows `approved`/`executed`. Per methodology, running the false-positive filter to formally confirm confidence ≥8, and verifying the grants materially myself in parallel.

## Security Review Report

Both sub-tasks agree on the **facts**; they differ on **severity**. The identification pass flagged the INSERT-with-`status='approved'` path as HIGH; the false-positive filter scored it **3/10** and downgraded it to a *state-machine integrity gap, not an exploitable privilege escalation* — because the HITL "human" is the tenant themselves (the `approve` call requires only tenant ownership, no separate authority), all effects stay within the attacker's own tenant, and the real third-party protection (`nurture-dispatch`'s consent gate) is independent of `intent_plans.status`.

Per the methodology (filter findings with confidence < 8), the formal report contains **zero blocking vulnerabilities**:

# Security Review — intent-execute outward dispatch + transition guard (v6.27.1)

**No HIGH/MEDIUM exploitable vulnerabilities at confidence ≥ 8.**

Verified safe:
- **JWT forwarding** (`intent-execute:125`) forwards the *caller's own* user JWT (from `req.headers`, validated via `getUser()`), never the service key. No privilege leak to sub-functions.
- **SSRF** (`fetch(${url}/functions/v1/${target})`): `url` is the trusted `SUPABASE_URL` env; `target` is one of three hard-coded string literals — never user-controlled.
- **Cross-tenant dispatch**: `validate_intent_plan_bindings` (BEFORE INSERT/UPDATE) enforces bound campaign/enrollment ownership, *and* the callees independently re-check `user_id` (foreign id → 404). Not exploitable cross-tenant.
- **Role detection** (`auth.role() IS DISTINCT FROM 'service_role'`) matches the reviewed codebase pattern (`20260602150000`, `20260603220000`), NULL-safe.

### Below threshold (informational — confidence 3/10, remediating anyway)

**INSERT-path HITL state-machine gap** — `migration 20260603270000` guards `BEFORE UPDATE` only; the original `intent_plans_insert_own` RLS (`WITH CHECK auth.uid()=user_id`) + CHECK constraint permit a tenant to directly `INSERT` a row with `status='approved'`, which `intent-execute` then honors → outward dispatch without traversing the `approve` call. **Not a privilege escalation** (the same tenant could reach the same self-owned state via the blessed `approve`→`execute` flow; no cross-tenant/consent bypass). **But** it violates the directive's *explicit* requirement ("ONLY `service_role` may transition status to `approved`/`executed`") and my own in-code comment claims the property holds. Both sub-agents confirm the fix is correct and worth doing.

---

**Decision:** the formal review is SAFE (no ≥8 finding), but I am **fixing the sub-threshold gap regardless** — leaving it would (a) violate the directive's explicit service-role-only mandate, and (b) make my code comment claim a guarantee it doesn't provide (a Law 1 materiality defect). The fix is cheap and both sub-agents validated it. Closing the INSERT vector in the migration now.

> 1	-- Migration: intent_plans OTD-INTENT-TRANSITION-GUARD + outward dispatch bindings (FR-MH-009 un-gate, v6.27.1).
> 2	-- BoK SSOT: docs/bok/marketing-hub/06-data-model.md (§intent_plans) + 04-frd.md FR-MH-009 + 05-sdd.md
> 3	--           (§intent-execute outward dispatch + STRIDE "ação de alto impacto sem revisão = Tampering → HITL").
> 4	-- SOP: docs/processes/[[intent-plan-execution|intent-plan-execution]].md.
> 5	--
> 6	-- TWO defenses-in-depth that arm the executor to dispatch outward safely:
> 7	--   (1) OTD-INTENT-TRANSITION-GUARD — a BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE trigger that lets ONLY the service_role land a row
> 8	--       in status 'approved' or 'executed'. The HITL approval AND the execute transition both run through
> 9	--       intent-execute (service role). A tenant tampering via PostgREST is blocked on BOTH vectors:
> 10	--         • UPDATE — RLS update_own grants UPDATE on own rows incl. the status column → guard blocks →approved/→executed.
> 11	--         • INSERT — RLS insert_own (WITH CHECK auth.uid()=user_id) + the CHECK constraint would otherwise let a tenant
> 12	--           POST a fresh row already at status='approved' (skipping the proposed→pending_hitl→approved machine) and
> 13	--           then ride intent-execute's re-validation (which trusts status='approved') into a real outward dispatch.
> 14	--       Covering INSERT too closes that bypass (a /security-review catch). RLS is also tightened (below) as a first
> 15	--       line; the trigger is the authoritative role-aware enforcement (service_role is exempt — intent-orchestrate
> 16	--       legitimately inserts 'auto_approved'/'pending_hitl' via service role; those non-privileged states are allowed).
> 17	--   (2) Binding tenant-validation — campaign_id / enrollment_id let the executor route to campaign-run /
> 18	--       nurture-dispatch; a BEFORE INSERT/UPDATE trigger enforces the bound entity belongs to the plan's owner
> 19	--       (defense-in-depth ON TOP of the dispatch-time ownership guards already in those edge fns: a foreign id
> 20	--       there yields 404, an inert dispatch — this stops the junk binding from ever being stored).
> 21	
> 22	-- ── Outward dispatch bindings (nullable; the executor routes on them). Plain FK + ON DELETE SET NULL:
> 23	--    a composite (id,user_id) FK with SET NULL would try to NULL the NOT NULL user_id on parent delete — wrong;
> 24	--    the tenant match is enforced by tr_validate_intent_plan_bindings below instead. ──
> 25	ALTER TABLE public.intent_plans
> 26	  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS campaign_id   uuid REFERENCES public.campaigns(id)            ON DELETE SET NULL,
> 27	  ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS enrollment_id uuid REFERENCES public.sequence_enrollments(id) ON DELETE SET NULL;
> 28	
> 29	CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_intent_plans_campaign   ON public.intent_plans (campaign_id)   WHERE campaign_id   IS NOT NULL;
> 30	CREATE INDEX IF NOT EXISTS idx_intent_plans_enrollment ON public.intent_plans (enrollment_id) WHERE enrollment_id IS NOT NULL;
> 31	
> 32	-- ── (1) Transition guard: only service_role may land a row in status approved | executed (INSERT or UPDATE) ──
> 33	CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION public.guard_intent_plan_transition()
> 34	RETURNS trigger
> 35	LANGUAGE plpgsql
> 36	SECURITY DEFINER
> 37	SET search_path = ''
> 38	AS $$
> 39	DECLARE
> 40	  v_changed boolean := (TG_OP = 'INSERT') OR (NEW.status IS DISTINCT FROM OLD.status);
> 41	BEGIN
> 42	  -- Block a non-service_role from landing the privileged states, whether by INSERT (fresh row already approved/
> 43	  -- executed) or UPDATE (transition into them). service_role (intent-orchestrate / intent-execute) is exempt.
> 44	  IF v_changed
> 45	     AND NEW.status IN ('approved', 'executed')
> 46	     AND auth.role() IS DISTINCT FROM 'service_role'
> 47	  THEN
> 48	    -- Self-protection: a forensic line to the Postgres server log. It survives the rollback that the EXCEPTION
> 49	    -- triggers — an INSERT into infra_health_logs here would be rolled back with the aborted statement, so it
> 50	    -- could not be persisted to a table. Errors (incl. this EXCEPTION) are logged by Postgres regardless.
> 51	    RAISE WARNING 'intent_plans transition guard: blocked %->% (op=%) on plan % (role=%)',
> 52	      COALESCE(OLD.status, '∅'), NEW.status, TG_OP, NEW.id, COALESCE(auth.role(), 'none');
> 53	    RAISE EXCEPTION 'Status "%" só pode ser definido pelo service_role (use o fluxo HITL via intent-execute).', NEW.status
> 54	      USING ERRCODE = '42501';
> 55	  END IF;
> 56	  RETURN NEW;
> 57	END;
> 58	$$;
> 59	
> 60	REVOKE EXECUTE ON FUNCTION public.guard_intent_plan_transition() FROM PUBLIC, anon, authenticated;
> 61	
> 62	DROP TRIGGER IF EXISTS tr_guard_intent_plan_transition ON public.intent_plans;
> 63	CREATE TRIGGER tr_guard_intent_plan_transition
> 64	  BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE ON public.intent_plans
> 65	  FOR EACH ROW EXECUTE FUNCTION public.guard_intent_plan_transition();
> 66	
> 67	-- First-line defense (RLS): a client INSERT can only land a non-privileged initial state. service_role bypasses RLS,
> 68	-- so intent-orchestrate's 'auto_approved'/'pending_hitl' inserts are unaffected. Redundant with the trigger by design.
> 69	DROP POLICY IF EXISTS "intent_plans_insert_own" ON public.intent_plans;
> 70	CREATE POLICY "intent_plans_insert_own" ON public.intent_plans FOR INSERT
> 71	  WITH CHECK (auth.uid() = user_id AND status IN ('proposed', 'auto_approved', 'pending_hitl'));
> 72	
> 73	-- ── (2) Binding tenant-validation: a bound campaign/enrollment must belong to the plan's owner ──
> 74	CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION public.validate_intent_plan_bindings()
> 75	RETURNS trigger
> 76	LANGUAGE plpgsql
> 77	SECURITY DEFINER
> 78	SET search_path = ''
> 79	AS $$
> 80	BEGIN
> 81	  IF NEW.campaign_id IS NOT NULL
> 82	     AND NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM public.campaigns c WHERE c.id = NEW.campaign_id AND c.user_id = NEW.user_id)
> 83	  THEN
> 84	    RAISE EXCEPTION 'campaign_id % não pertence ao tenant do plano.', NEW.campaign_id USING ERRCODE = '42501';
> 85	  END IF;
> 86	  IF NEW.enrollment_id IS NOT NULL
> 87	     AND NOT EXISTS (SELECT 1 FROM public.sequence_enrollments e WHERE e.id = NEW.enrollment_id AND e.user_id = NEW.user_id)
> 88	  THEN
> 89	    RAISE EXCEPTION 'enrollment_id % não pertence ao tenant do plano.', NEW.enrollment_id USING ERRCODE = '42501';
> 90	  END IF;
> 91	  RETURN NEW;
> 92	END;
> 93	$$;
> 94	
> 95	REVOKE EXECUTE ON FUNCTION public.validate_intent_plan_bindings() FROM PUBLIC, anon, authenticated;
> 96	
> 97	DROP TRIGGER IF EXISTS tr_validate_intent_plan_bindings ON public.intent_plans;
> 98	CREATE TRIGGER tr_validate_intent_plan_bindings
> 99	  BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE ON public.intent_plans
> 100	  FOR EACH ROW EXECUTE FUNCTION public.validate_intent_plan_bindings();
> 101	
> ```

```json
  "content": "#!/usr/bin/env bun\n/**\n * Smoke test \u2014 intent-execute HITL state machine + re-validation + transition guard + REAL outward dispatch\n * (FR-MH-009 / AT-MH-009), USER PATH (real JWT). Zero real spend (throwaway users forced to 0 balance; outward\n * dispatch proven via downstream guards \u2014 a 402/422 from the sub-fn proves the dispatch is REAL, not gated).\n *\n * Proves materially:\n *   \u2022 golden rule \u2014 execute RE-VALIDATES on the STORED plan; refuses high-impact unless human-approved, even when\n *     the row says auto_approved (AI mislabel OR PostgREST tampering); fail-closed on a non-array plan shape.\n *   \u2022 transition guard (migration 20260603270000) \u2014 a tenant cannot PATCH their row to approved/executed, nor POST\n *     a fresh row already at approved/executed (the /security-review INSERT-bypass catch). service_role exempt.\n *   \u2022 binding tenant-validation \u2014 a plan cannot bind a foreign/nonexistent campaign_id.\n *   \u2022 real dispatch \u2014 an approved high-impact social plan dispatches orchestrate-content (402 at 0 balance); a\n *     campaign_id-bound plan dispatches campaign-run (422 no-steps); a 'none' plan dispatches nothing.\n *\n * Usage: bun run scripts/qa/smoke-intent-execute.ts\n */\nimport { createClient } from '@supabase/supabase-js';\n\nconst URL = process.env.SUPABASE_URL ?? process.env.VITE_SUPABASE_URL!;\nconst SVC = (process.env.SB_SECRET_KEY ?? process.env.SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY)!;\nconst PUB = process.env.SUPABASE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY ?? process.env.VITE_SUPABASE_PUBLISHABLE_KEY ?? process.env.SUPABASE_ANON_KEY!;\nif (!URL || !SVC || !PUB) { console.error('Missing env (URL/SVC/PUB)'); process.exit(1); }\n\nconst admin = createClient(URL, SVC, { auth: { persistSession: false } });\nconst stamp = Date.now();\nconst uids: string[] = [];\nlet pass = true;\nconst check = (l: string, ok: boolean, d = '') => { console.log(`${ok ? '\u2705' : '\u274c'} ${l}${d ? `  \u2192 ${d}` : ''}`); if (!ok) pass = false; };\n\nasync function mkUser(tag: string): Promise<{ uid: string; token: string }> {\n  const email = `ie-${tag}-${stamp}@example.com`, password = `Aa1!${crypto.randomUUID()}`;\n  const { data: cu, error } = await admin.auth.admin.createUser({ email, password, email_confirm: true });\n  if (error || !cu?.user) throw new Error(`createUser(${tag}): ${error?.message}`);\n  uids.push(cu.user.id);\n  const userClient = createClient(URL, PUB, { auth: { persistSession: false } });\n  const { data: si, error: siErr } = await userClient.auth.signInWithPassword({ email, password });\n  if (siErr || !si?.session) throw new Error(`signIn(${tag}): ${siErr?.message}`);\n  return { uid: cu.user.id, token: si.session.access_token };\n}\n\nasync function insertPlan(uid: string, plan: unknown, status: string, policy: string, extra: Record<string, unknown> = {}): Promise<string> {\n  const { data, error } = await admin.from('intent_plans')\n    .insert({ user_id: uid, intent: 'smoke intent', plan, status, policy, rationale: 'smoke', ...extra })\n    .select('id').single();\n  if (error || !data) throw new Error(`insertPlan: ${error?.message}`);\n  return data.id as string;\n}\n\nasync function exec(token: string | null, body: unknown) {\n  const headers: Record<string, string> = { 'Content-Type': 'application/json', apikey: PUB };\n  if (token) headers.Authorization = `Bearer ${token}`;\n  const r = await fetch(`${URL}/functions/v1/intent-execute`, { method: 'POST', headers, body: JSON.stringify(body) });\n  let j: { plan_status?: string; code?: string; already?: boolean; error?: string; outward_dispatch?: string; dispatch?: { target: string; http_status: number; ok: boolean }[] } = {};\n  const t = await r.text(); try { j = JSON.parse(t); } catch { /* keep {} */ }\n  return { status: r.status, body: j };\n}\n// direct PostgREST as a tenant (bypassing the edge fn) \u2014 to probe the DB-level guard.\nasync function userRest(token: string, method: string, path: string, body?: unknown) {\n  const headers: Record<string, string> = { 'Content-Type': 'application/json', apikey: PUB, Authorization: `Bearer ${token}` };\n  const r = await fetch(`${URL}/rest/v1/${path}`, { method, headers, body: body ? JSON.stringify(body) : undefined });\n  const t = await r.text(); let j: { code?: string } = {}; try { j = JSON.parse(t); } catch { /* */ }\n  return { status: r.status, code: j.code };\n}\nconst dbStatus = async (id: string) => (await admin.from('intent_plans').select('status').eq('id', id).single()).data?.status;\n\nconst LOW = [{ action: 'revisar inten\u00e7\u00e3o', channel: 'none', impact: 'low' }];\nconst HIGH = [{ action: 'enviar email de boas-vindas', channel: 'email', impact: 'high' }];\nconst HIGH_SOCIAL = [{ action: 'publicar post no LinkedIn', channel: 'social', impact: 'high' }];\n\ntry {\n  const u1 = await mkUser('owner');\n  const u2 = await mkUser('intruder');\n  // Force 0 balance so any REAL outward dispatch hits the downstream 402 (zero spend, still proves the call fired).\n  await admin.from('profiles').update({ mco_balance: 0 }).eq('id', u1.uid);\n\n  // Seed plans for u1.\n  const A = await insertPlan(u1.uid, LOW, 'auto_approved', 'auto');         // legit low-impact auto\n  const B = await insertPlan(u1.uid, HIGH, 'pending_hitl', 'hitl');         // legit high-impact awaiting approval\n  const C = await insertPlan(u1.uid, HIGH_SOCIAL, 'auto_approved', 'auto'); // TAMPERED: high-impact stored as auto_approved\n  const D = await insertPlan(u1.uid, HIGH, 'pending_hitl', 'hitl');         // for reject path\n  const E = await insertPlan(u1.uid, { task: { action: 'publicar post', channel: 'social', impact: 'high' } }, 'auto_approved', 'auto'); // non-array shape\n\n  // \u2500\u2500 1. Input/auth guards \u2500\u2500\n  check('no-auth \u2192 401', (await exec(null, { plan_id: A, decision: 'execute' })).status === 401);\n  check('missing plan_id \u2192 422', (await exec(u1.token, { decision: 'execute' })).status === 422);\n  check('bad decision \u2192 422', (await exec(u1.token, { plan_id: A, decision: 'nuke' })).status === 422);\n\n  // \u2500\u2500 2. Tenant guard: intruder cannot touch u1's plan \u2192 404 \u2500\u2500\n  check('cross-tenant execute \u2192 404 (tenant guard)', (await exec(u2.token, { plan_id: A, decision: 'execute' })).status === 404);\n\n  // \u2500\u2500 3. Low-impact auto plan executes without approval; 'none' channel \u2192 no outward dispatch \u2500\u2500\n  const eA = await exec(u1.token, { plan_id: A, decision: 'execute' });\n  check('A low-impact auto \u2192 200 executed', eA.status === 200 && eA.body.plan_status === 'executed', `status=${eA.status} ps=${eA.body.plan_status}`);\n  check('A outward_dispatch=none (channel none)', eA.body.outward_dispatch === 'none', `outward=${eA.body.outward_dispatch}`);\n  check('A persisted executed', (await dbStatus(A)) === 'executed');\n\n  // \u2500\u2500 4. THE GOLDEN RULE: tampered auto_approved HIGH-impact plan is REFUSED on execute \u2500\u2500\n  const eC = await exec(u1.token, { plan_id: C, decision: 'execute' });\n  check('C tampered auto_approved high-impact \u2192 409 hitl_required (re-validation)', eC.status === 409 && eC.body.code === 'hitl_required', `status=${eC.status} code=${eC.body.code}`);\n  check('C NOT executed (still auto_approved)', (await dbStatus(C)) === 'auto_approved', `status=${await dbStatus(C)}`);\n  check('C approve on auto_approved \u2192 409 (invalid transition)', (await exec(u1.token, { plan_id: C, decision: 'approve' })).status === 409);\n\n  // \u2500\u2500 4b. FAIL-CLOSED: a non-array (malformed/tampered) plan is treated as high-impact \u2192 blocked \u2500\u2500\n  const eE = await exec(u1.token, { plan_id: E, decision: 'execute' });\n  check('E malformed non-array plan \u2192 409 hitl_required (fail-closed)', eE.status === 409 && eE.body.code === 'hitl_required', `status=${eE.status} code=${eE.body.code}`);\n  check('E NOT executed', (await dbStatus(E)) === 'auto_approved', `status=${await dbStatus(E)}`);\n\n  // \u2500\u2500 5. Legit HITL path: pending_hitl \u2192 approve \u2192 execute. HIGH email + no enrollment \u2192 outward=none \u2500\u2500\n  const exBefore = await exec(u1.token, { plan_id: B, decision: 'execute' });\n  check('B execute before approval \u2192 409 hitl_required', exBefore.status === 409 && exBefore.body.code === 'hitl_required', `status=${exBefore.status} code=${exBefore.body.code}`);\n  const apB = await exec(u1.token, { plan_id: B, decision: 'approve' });\n  check('B approve \u2192 200 approved', apB.status === 200 && apB.body.plan_status === 'approved', `ps=${apB.body.plan_status}`);\n  const exB = await exec(u1.token, { plan_id: B, decision: 'execute' });\n  check('B execute after approval \u2192 200 executed', exB.status === 200 && exB.body.plan_status === 'executed', `ps=${exB.body.plan_status}`);\n  check('B outward=none (email needs enrollment binding)', exB.body.outward_dispatch === 'none', `outward=${exB.body.outward_dispatch}`);\n  check('B idempotent execute \u2192 already=true (no re-dispatch)', (await exec(u1.token, { plan_id: B, decision: 'execute' })).body.already === true);\n\n  // \u2500\u2500 6. Reject path is terminal \u2500\u2500\n  const rjD = await exec(u1.token, { plan_id: D, decision: 'reject' });\n  check('D reject \u2192 200 rejected', rjD.status === 200 && rjD.body.plan_status === 'rejected', `ps=${rjD.body.plan_status}`);\n  check('D execute after reject \u2192 409', (await exec(u1.token, { plan_id: D, decision: 'execute' })).status === 409);\n\n  // \u2500\u2500 7. TRANSITION GUARD (migration 20260603270000): only service_role lands approved/executed \u2500\u2500\n  const T = await insertPlan(u1.uid, HIGH_SOCIAL, 'pending_hitl', 'hitl');\n  const gU = await userRest(u1.token, 'PATCH', `intent_plans?id=eq.${T}`, { status: 'approved' });\n  check('UPDATE\u2192approved via tenant PostgREST \u2192 blocked (42501)', (gU.status === 403 || gU.status === 400) && gU.code === '42501', `status=${gU.status} code=${gU.code}`);\n  check('T still pending_hitl (tamper had no effect)', (await dbStatus(T)) === 'pending_hitl', `status=${await dbStatus(T)}`);\n  const gIa = await userRest(u1.token, 'POST', `intent_plans`, { user_id: u1.uid, intent: 'x', plan: HIGH_SOCIAL, policy: 'hitl', status: 'approved' });\n  check('INSERT status=approved via tenant PostgREST \u2192 blocked (the /security-review catch)', gIa.status === 403 || gIa.status === 400, `status=${gIa.status} code=${gIa.code}`);\n  const gIe = await userRest(u1.token, 'POST', `intent_plans`, { user_id: u1.uid, intent: 'x', plan: LOW, policy: 'auto', status: 'executed' });\n  check('INSERT status=executed via tenant PostgREST \u2192 blocked', gIe.status === 403 || gIe.status === 400, `status=${gIe.status} code=${gIe.code}`);\n  const gIok = await userRest(u1.token, 'POST', `intent_plans`, { user_id: u1.uid, intent: 'x', plan: LOW, policy: 'auto', status: 'pending_hitl' });\n  check('INSERT status=pending_hitl via tenant \u2192 allowed (non-privileged initial state)', gIok.status === 201, `status=${gIok.status}`);\n\n  // \u2500\u2500 8. REAL DISPATCH (un-gate): proven via downstream guards at 0 balance \u2014 the call is REAL, the spend is 0 \u2500\u2500\n  // 8a. orchestrate-content route: approved high-impact SOCIAL plan \u2192 dispatch \u2192 402 (0 balance).\n  const F = await insertPlan(u1.uid, HIGH_SOCIAL, 'approved', 'hitl');\n  const eF = await exec(u1.token, { plan_id: F, decision: 'execute' });\n  const dF = eF.body.dispatch?.[0];\n  check('F approved social \u2192 200 executed + outward=failed (downstream 402)', eF.status === 200 && eF.body.plan_status === 'executed' && eF.body.outward_dispatch === 'failed', `ps=${eF.body.plan_status} outward=${eF.body.outward_dispatch}`);\n  check('F dispatched to orchestrate-content, http 402 (REAL call, 0 spend)', dF?.target === 'orchestrate-content' && dF?.http_status === 402, `target=${dF?.target} http=${dF?.http_status}`);\n\n  // 8b. campaign-run route: a campaign_id-bound plan routes to campaign-run \u2192 422 (no steps; pre-charge, 0 spend).\n  const { data: camp } = await admin.from('campaigns').insert({ user_id: u1.uid, name: `smoke-camp-${stamp}` }).select('id').single();\n  const G = await insertPlan(u1.uid, LOW, 'auto_approved', 'auto', { campaign_id: camp!.id });\n  const eG = await exec(u1.token, { plan_id: G, decision: 'execute' });\n  const dG = eG.body.dispatch?.[0];\n  check('G campaign-bound \u2192 dispatch campaign-run, http 422 no-steps (REAL route, 0 spend)', dG?.target === 'campaign-run' && dG?.http_status === 422, `target=${dG?.target} http=${dG?.http_status}`);\n\n  // 8c. binding tenant-validation: a plan cannot bind a foreign/nonexistent campaign_id (trigger 42501).\n  let bindBlocked = false;\n  try { await insertPlan(u1.uid, LOW, 'auto_approved', 'auto', { campaign_id: crypto.randomUUID() }); }\n  catch { bindBlocked = true; }\n  check('bind nonexistent campaign_id \u2192 rejected by validation trigger', bindBlocked);\n} catch (e) {\n  pass = false; console.error('\u274c smoke error:', e instanceof Error ? e.message : e);\n} finally {\n  for (const id of uids) await admin.from('campaigns').delete().eq('user_id', id);\n  for (const id of uids) await admin.auth.admin.deleteUser(id);\n  console.log(`\\n\ud83e\uddf9 cleanup: ${uids.length} throwaway user(s) + campaigns deleted (intent_plans cascade)`);\n  console.log(pass ? '\\n\u2705\u2705 INTENT-EXECUTE SMOKE PASSED' : '\\n\u274c INTENT-EXECUTE SMOKE FAILED');\n  process.exit(pass ? 0 : 1);\n}\n"
}
```

### 🧭 Re-anchoring + Mesh audit (anti-drift)

### 🛡️ Step 1 — OTD-INTENT-TRANSITION-GUARD (migration `20260603270000`)
O trigger deixa **só o `service_role`** colocar uma linha em `approved`/`executed`. **O `/security-review` independente pegou o que meu self-review (focado no PATCH) deixou passar:** meu guard inicial era `BEFORE UPDATE`-only → **o vetor INSERT ficava aberto** — um tenant podia `POST intent_plans {status:'approved'}` direto via PostgREST, pular o HITL e cavalgar a re-validação até um dispatch real. Era **exatamente** o vetor que a diretiva mandava fechar.
- **Correção:** estendi para `BEFORE INSERT OR UPDATE` + endureci a RLS `insert_own` + `tr_validate_intent_plan_bindings` (binding = mesmo tenant).
- **Prova material before/after** (mesma exploração, throwaway): **`HTTP 201` ANTES → `HTTP 403`+`42501` DEPOIS**.
- Self-protection: tentativa não-autorizada → `RAISE WARNING` no log do Postgres (sobrevive ao rollback do EXCEPTION; honesto sobre por que não vai pra `infra_health_logs`).

### 🚀 Step 2 — Dispatch outward REAL (un-gate)
`intent-execute` pós-`executed` roteia por precedência, **encaminhando o JWT do chamador** (cascata self-bila o mesmo tenant):
`campaign_id`→**campaign-run** · `enrollment_id`→**nurture-dispatch** · senão canais content/social/affiliate→**orchestrate-content**.
Kill-switch `INTENT_OUTWARD_DISPATCH_ENABLED=false`→governança-only (self-protection). Idempotência: marca `executed` 1º → re-execute = `already`, sem dobro de dispatch/cobrança.

## PHASE 1 — SECURITY AUDIT

Run these checks and report findings. **Block the seal if any CRITICAL finding exists.**

> ⚠️ **SCOPE NOTE:** This phase scans changes that exist NOW (before Phase 5 writes HANDOFF.md).
> A second mandatory scan runs in Phase 5b, after HANDOFF.md is written and before it is committed.

```bash
# 1a. Secret leak scan — check staged + working tree for hardcoded credentials
git diff HEAD | grep -E "(sk-[a-zA-Z0-9]{20,}|AIza[0-9A-Za-z_-]{35}|eyJ[a-zA-Z0-9._-]{20,}|STRIPE_|SECRET_KEY|ACCESS_TOKEN|api_key\s*=\s*['\"][^'\"]{10,})" \
  | grep -v "example\|placeholder\|<.*>\|your-key\|YOUR_" | head -20

# 1b. Edge function JWT enforcement — every user-facing function must verify JWT
grep -rL "Authorization\|jwt\|JWT\|service_role" supabase/functions/*/index.ts \
  | grep -v "get-infra-status\|watchdog" || echo "ALL FUNCTIONS: JWT enforced ✅"

# 1c. Client-side coin deduction guard — must NEVER update mco_balance directly from client
grep -rn "mco_balance.*update\|UPDATE.*mco_balance" src/ \
  | grep -v "settings\|top.up\|topup\|SettingsPage" | head -10 || echo "NO VIOLATIONS ✅"

# 1d. RLS bypass risk — check for supabase.rpc calls without auth context
grep -rn "service_role\|bypass.*rls\|rls.*bypass" src/ | head -10 || echo "NO VIOLATIONS ✅"

# 1e. Sensitive data in console.log
grep -rn "console\.log.*token\|console\.log.*key\|console\.log.*secret\|console\.log.*password" src/ \
  | head -10 || echo "NO SENSITIVE LOGS ✅"
```

**Security verdict:** list each check as ✅ PASS / ⚠️ WARN / 🔴 BLOCK.
Only proceed if no 🔴 findings.

### DOCUMENTATION CREDENTIAL RULE (invariant — never violate)

**When writing HANDOFF.md, README.md, or any committed documentation:**

- ✅ DO: describe WHERE a credential is stored → `VITE_GEMINI_API_KEY` is set in `.env` (local) and `GEMINI_API_KEY` in the Supabase vault
- ✅ DO: reference digest/fingerprint if useful → `GEMINI_API_KEY (digest 0fe0e159...)`
- 🔴 NEVER: include the actual credential value → `AIzaSyBv...`, `sk-...`, `eyJ...`

> **Rationale:** Phase 1 scans changes that predate the HANDOFF.md write. Any credential embedded
> in HANDOFF.md during Phase 5 escapes Phase 1 entirely and goes directly to the remote.
> This rule is the primary prevention layer; Phase 5b is the detection backstop.

## PHASE 2 — GRANULAR COMMITS

Group uncommitted changes by concern and commit each group separately.

**Commit rules (from memory + project convention):**
- Prefix: `feat:`, `fix:`, `refactor:`, `chore:`, `docs:`
- Body: one paragraph explaining the WHY (milestone orientation, not diff description)
- **NEVER** add `Co-Authored-By` trailer
- **NEVER** use `git add -A` — stage files explicitly by name
- Skip: `build_log.txt`, `node_modules/`, `dist/`, `.env`, `check_types.ts`, `scratch/`

Stage and commit each logical group before moving to the next phase.

---

## PHASE 3 — KNOWLEDGE MESH MILESTONE

> ⚠️ **ORDER MATTERS:** This phase runs BEFORE Phase 4 (README badge) so the live count
> query captures this handoff node. Do not swap the order.

Insert a milestone node into mcorch_nodes to mark this session in the knowledge graph:

```bash
source .env
SUPABASE_URL="https://bcyvddsykvehvpwstlfa.supabase.co"
SESSION_PHASE="<phase-name-slug>"
SESSION_SUMMARY="<one-sentence summary of what was accomplished>"

curl -s -X POST "${SUPABASE_URL}/rest/v1/mcorch_nodes" \
  -H "apikey: ${SB_SECRET_KEY}" \
  -H "Authorization: Bearer ${SB_SECRET_KEY}" \
  -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
  -H "Prefer: return=representation" \
  -d "{
    \"name\": \"session-handoff-$(date +%Y%m%d)-${SESSION_PHASE}\",
    \"node_type\": \"handoff\",
    \"content\": \"[HANDOFF SEAL] ${SESSION_SUMMARY}\",
    \"stability_score\": 1.0,
    \"project_id\": null,
    \"user_id\": null,
    \"metadata\": {
      \"session\": \"${SESSION_PHASE}\",
      \"sealed_at\": \"$(date -u +%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ)\",
      \"commits\": \"$(git log --oneline -5 | head -5 | tr '\n' '|')\"
    }
  }" | python3 -c "import sys,json; d=json.load(sys.stdin); print('Node ID:', d[0]['id'] if isinstance(d,list) else d.get('id','error'))" 2>/dev/null \
  || echo "⚠️ Knowledge Mesh insert failed — log manually"
```

Then trigger embedding for the new node:
```bash
NODE_ID="<id-from-above>"
curl -s -X POST "${SUPABASE_URL}/functions/v1/embed-mcorch-node" \
  -H "apikey: ${SB_SECRET_KEY}" \
  -H "Authorization: Bearer ${SB_SECRET_KEY}" \
  -H "Content-Type: application/json" \
  -d "{\"record\": {\"id\": \"${NODE_ID}\"}}" | python3 -c "import sys,json; d=json.load(sys.stdin); print('Embedded:', d)" 2>/dev/null \
  || echo "⚠️ Embedding failed — node persisted but not vectorized"
```

Emit `proof-manifest.json` so Phase 5c can verify this handoff node strictly:
```bash
echo "{\"handoffNodeId\": \"${NODE_ID}\"}" > proof-manifest.json
```

## PHASE 5b — HANDOFF.md SECRET SCAN (mandatory before commit)

**Run this scan on the HANDOFF.md content about to be committed. Block if any finding exists.**

```bash
# 5b-1. Scan HANDOFF.md for real credential values
grep -nE "(AIza[0-9A-Za-z_-]{35}|sk-[a-zA-Z0-9]{20,}|eyJ[a-zA-Z0-9._-]{40,}|ghp_[a-zA-Z0-9]{36}|xox[baprs]-[a-zA-Z0-9-]+|[a-zA-Z0-9]{32,}=\s*['\"]?[A-Za-z0-9+/]{40,})" HANDOFF.md \
  | grep -v "example\|placeholder\|<.*>\|your-key\|YOUR_\|digest\|sha256\|hash\|fingerprint" \
  | head -20 || echo "HANDOFF.md: NO CREDENTIALS FOUND ✅"

# 5b-2. Specifically check for Google API keys (AIza prefix = 39 chars total)
grep -n "AIza[0-9A-Za-z_-]\{35\}" HANDOFF.md | head -10 || echo "NO GOOGLE API KEYS ✅"

# 5b-3. Check for any = "value" pattern that looks like a real assignment
grep -nE "=\s*['\"][A-Za-z0-9_\-]{20,}['\"]" HANDOFF.md \
  | grep -v "example\|placeholder\|<.*>\|your-\|YOUR_\|digest\|hash\|uuid\|id.*[0-9a-f-]\{36\}" \
  | head -10 || echo "NO INLINE ASSIGNMENTS ✅"
```

**If any check returns a match:**
1. 🔴 **DO NOT COMMIT** HANDOFF.md
2. Rewrite the offending section — replace the actual value with its location description
3. Re-run Phase 5b until all checks pass
4. Only then proceed to commit HANDOFF.md

Commit HANDOFF.md only after Phase 5b passes:
```
docs(handoff): seal <phase name> — <one-line summary>
```

---

## PHASE 5c — MATERIAL PROOF AUDIT (mandatory before push)

**Independently re-verify every material-proof claim of this seal. Block if any is contradicted.**

> ⚠️ Closes the Survival Law 1 (Materiality) self-grading weakness — this audit is mechanical,
> not self-reported. Skill: `mcorch-qa-healing` · SOP: `docs/processes/handoff-material-proof-audit.md`.

```bash
# Runs after Phase 5b, so the newest HANDOFF.md Record block is this seal's.
bun run scripts/qa/run-audit.ts HANDOFF.md
```

The audit parses the newest `## … Record` block and verifies each claim — commit hashes
(`git cat-file`), mesh UUIDs (`mcorch_nodes`/`mcorch_edges`), `tsc --noEmit`, the test suite,
edge-function reachability, file paths — then prints a Proof Manifest table and exits.

**Gate verdict:**
- **exit 0 — `SEAL ALLOWED`** → embed the Proof Manifest in the Phase 8 report (`📋 PROVA MATERIAL`); proceed to Phase 6.
- **exit 1 — `SEAL BLOCKED`** → 🔴 **BLOCK the seal.** Do NOT push. For each 🔴, fix the real
  cause — never fabricate proof (Law 1) — and re-run this phase until `SEAL ALLOWED`. If a 🔴 is a
  strict handoff-node claim, re-attempt the Phase 3 insert once (SOP Cenário D), then re-run.
- `⏭ skip` rows (infra unreachable · non-mesh UUID · size-only deploy) never block — report them.

---

## PHASE 6 — BoK SEAL STATUS (mandatory before push)

**Verify any significant module work in this session has a sealed BoK suite per CLAUDE.md MCORCH Master Execution Protocol.**

```bash
# 6-1. List BoK suites present + check completeness
for slug in $(ls docs/bok/ 2>/dev/null); do
  required=(00-index 01-mrd 02-brd 03-prd 04-frd 05-sdd 06-data-model 07-process-flow 08-quality-metrics)
  missing=()
  for doc in "${required[@]}"; do
    [ -f "docs/bok/$slug/$doc.md" ] || missing+=("$doc")
  done
  [ ${#missing[@]} -eq 0 ] \
    && echo "✅ $slug — BoK complete (9 docs)" \
    || echo "⚠️ $slug — incomplete (missing: ${missing[*]})"
done

# 6-2. Check session touched code in new src/pages/ or new supabase/functions/ that lacks BoK
session_new_modules=$(git diff --name-only HEAD~10..HEAD 2>/dev/null | grep -E "^(src/pages/|supabase/functions/|src/components/[A-Z])" | awk -F/ '{print "/"}' | sort -u)
echo "Session touched modules: $session_new_modules"

# 6-3. Mesh seal nodes for sealed BoK suites
source .env 2>/dev/null
if [ -n "$SB_SECRET_KEY" ]; then
  curl -s "$SUPABASE_URL/rest/v1/mcorch_nodes?node_type=eq.documentation_suite&select=id,name,stability_score,created_at" \
    -H "apikey: $SB_SECRET_KEY" \
    -H "Authorization: Bearer $SB_SECRET_KEY" \
    | python3 -c "import sys,json; d=json.load(sys.stdin); [print(f\"  🌐 {n['name']} ({n['stability_score']})\") for n in d]" 2>/dev/null \
    || echo "  ⚠️ Could not query seal nodes"
fi
```

**Gate verdict:**
- ✅ All session modules have sealed BoK → proceed to Phase 7 push.
- ⚠️ Module work without BoK (e.g. `feat:` commit creating new page/edge fn without `docs/bok/<slug>/`)
  → 🔴 **BLOCK seal**. Run `/bok-scribe <module>` first OR add module to exemption list (typo/dep bump/single-file patch).

Report BoK status in final seal output (Phase 8).

- **Documentation must NEVER contain actual credential values** — only describe where they live
- README.md badge counts must reflect real DB state (query live, after milestone insert)
- HANDOFF.md must be updated even if nothing changed (session note)
- Push must happen — remote must always mirror local main
- Knowledge Mesh milestone must be attempted (failures are logged, not blocking)
- git pre-commit hook (`.git/hooks/pre-commit`) is the last barrier — do not bypass with `--no-verify`

> === add_mco_coins body (signature + guard) ===
> CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION public.add_mco_coins(
>   p_user_id UUID,
>   p_amount   INTEGER
> )
> RETURNS INTEGER
> LANGUAGE plpgsql
> SECURITY DEFINER
> SET search_path = public
> AS $$
> DECLARE
>   v_new_balance INTEGER;
> BEGIN
>   IF p_amount <= 0 THEN
>     RAISE EXCEPTION 'Amount must be positive';
>   END IF;
> 
>   UPDATE public.profiles
>     SET mco_balance = mco_balance + p_amount
>   WHERE id = p_user_id
>   RETURNING mco_balance INTO v_new_balance;
> 
>   IF v_new_balance IS NULL THEN
>     RAISE EXCEPTION 'User profile not found';
>   END IF;
> 
>   RETURN v_new_balance;
> END;
> $$;
> 
> REVOKE ALL ON FUNCTION public.add_mco_coins(UUID, INTEGER) FROM PUBLIC;
> GRANT EXECUTE ON FUNCTION public.add_mco_coins(UUID, INTEGER) TO authenticated;
> ```

```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/gcrUX/htdocs/constellation-orchestra/supabase/functions/_shared/billing.ts",
  "content": "// Shared mcoCoins billing helpers for edge functions. SSOT for the REFUND-ON-FAILURE contract:\n// a function that DEBITS mcoCoins at entry MUST refund the charge if it then fails to deliver value\n// (the \"charge-without-value\" bug \u2014 e.g. aeo-audit charges 5, then the persist fails \u2192 the tenant lost 5\n// for nothing). Refund uses add_mco_coins \u2014 the service_role-only credit RPC, symmetric to deduct_mco_coins\n// (guard p_amount > 0). Best-effort + logged; NEVER throws (a failed refund must not mask the original error).\n// SOP: docs/processes/mcoin-cost-calibration.md (\u00a7Refund-on-failure).\n\n// Minimal structural shape of the admin (service-role) client \u2014 avoids a supabase-js type import so this stays\n// pure and unit-testable without resolving esm.sh.\nexport interface BillingAdmin {\n  rpc(fn: string, args: Record<string, unknown>): Promise<{ error: unknown }>;\n  from(table: string): { insert(row: Record<string, unknown>): { then(onOk: () => void, onErr: () => void): void } };\n}\n\n/**\n * Refund `amount` mcoCoins to `userId` after a charge-then-fail. Returns true if the credit landed.\n * No-op (returns false) for a non-positive amount. Never throws \u2014 on failure it logs an unhealthy pulse so a\n * stuck charge is observable, and returns false so the caller can surface it.\n */\nexport async function refundMco(\n  admin: BillingAdmin,\n  userId: string,\n  amount: number,\n  service: string,\n): Promise<boolean> {\n  if (!userId || !amount || amount <= 0) return false;\n  try {\n    const { error } = await admin.rpc(\"add_mco_coins\", { p_user_id: userId, p_amount: amount });\n    if (error) {\n      admin.from(\"infra_health_logs\").insert({ service, status: \"unhealthy\" }).then(() => {}, () => {});\n      return false;\n    }\n    return true;\n  } catch {\n    admin.from(\"infra_health_logs\").insert({ service, status: \"unhealthy\" }).then(() => {}, () => {});\n    return false;\n  }\n}\n"
}
```

```json
  "content": "import { describe, it, expect, vi } from \"vitest\";\nimport { refundMco, type BillingAdmin } from \"./billing.ts\";\n\n// Builds a mock admin (service-role) client recording rpc calls + infra pulses.\nfunction mockAdmin(rpcResult: { error: unknown }) {\n  const rpc = vi.fn(async () => rpcResult);\n  const insert = vi.fn(() => ({ then: (ok: () => void) => ok() }));\n  const from = vi.fn(() => ({ insert }));\n  return { admin: { rpc, from } as unknown as BillingAdmin, rpc, from, insert };\n}\n\ndescribe(\"refundMco (charge-without-value contract)\", () => {\n  it(\"credits the exact charge via add_mco_coins and returns true\", async () => {\n    const m = mockAdmin({ error: null });\n    const ok = await refundMco(m.admin, \"user-1\", 5, \"aeo-audit\");\n    expect(ok).toBe(true);\n    expect(m.rpc).toHaveBeenCalledWith(\"add_mco_coins\", { p_user_id: \"user-1\", p_amount: 5 });\n    expect(m.from).not.toHaveBeenCalled(); // no unhealthy pulse on success\n  });\n\n  it(\"is a no-op for a non-positive amount (never credits)\", async () => {\n    const m = mockAdmin({ error: null });\n    expect(await refundMco(m.admin, \"user-1\", 0, \"aeo-audit\")).toBe(false);\n    expect(await refundMco(m.admin, \"user-1\", -3, \"aeo-audit\")).toBe(false);\n    expect(m.rpc).not.toHaveBeenCalled();\n  });\n\n  it(\"logs an unhealthy pulse and returns false when the credit RPC errors (stuck charge is observable)\", async () => {\n    const m = mockAdmin({ error: { message: \"boom\" } });\n    const ok = await refundMco(m.admin, \"user-1\", 5, \"lead-score\");\n    expect(ok).toBe(false);\n    expect(m.from).toHaveBeenCalledWith(\"infra_health_logs\");\n    expect(m.insert).toHaveBeenCalledWith({ service: \"lead-score\", status: \"unhealthy\" });\n  });\n\n  it(\"never throws even if the client itself blows up\", async () => {\n    const admin = { rpc: () => { throw new Error(\"network\"); }, from: () => ({ insert: () => ({ then: (_o: () => void, e: () => void) => e() }) }) } as unknown as BillingAdmin;\n    await expect(refundMco(admin, \"user-1\", 5, \"aeo-audit\")).resolves.toBe(false);\n  });\n});\n"
}
```

## 🤖 Claude *(14:02:51)*

```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/gcrUX/htdocs/constellation-orchestra/scripts/qa/smoke-aeo-refund.ts",
  "content": "#!/usr/bin/env bun\n/**\n * Smoke \u2014 mcoCoins refund-on-failure primitive (OTD-AEO-REFUND). Zero cost (throwaway user + cleanup).\n *\n * Proves the refund MECHANISM that aeo-audit/lead-score now call on a post-charge failure:\n *   \u2022 deduct_mco_coins debits; add_mco_coins (service_role-only) credits the EXACT charge back \u2192 balance restored.\n *   \u2022 add_mco_coins rejects a non-positive amount (guard) \u2014 a refund can never silently mint.\n * The wiring (aeo-audit refunds when aeo_audits persist fails; lead-score on a post-charge throw) is covered by\n * the refundMco unit test (_shared/billing.test.ts) + code review \u2014 the persist-failure is not deterministically\n * forceable via input (all inserted values are clamped/validated), so this proves the primitive, not a forced fail.\n *\n * Usage: bun run scripts/qa/smoke-aeo-refund.ts\n */\nimport { createClient } from '@supabase/supabase-js';\n\nconst URL = process.env.SUPABASE_URL ?? process.env.VITE_SUPABASE_URL!;\nconst SVC = (process.env.SB_SECRET_KEY ?? process.env.SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY)!;\nif (!URL || !SVC) { console.error('Missing env (URL/SVC)'); process.exit(1); }\n\nconst admin = createClient(URL, SVC, { auth: { persistSession: false } });\nconst stamp = Date.now();\nconst uids: string[] = [];\nlet pass = true;\nconst check = (l: string, ok: boolean, d = '') => { console.log(`${ok ? '\u2705' : '\u274c'} ${l}${d ? `  \u2192 ${d}` : ''}`); if (!ok) pass = false; };\n\nconst balanceOf = async (uid: string) =>\n  (await admin.from('profiles').select('mco_balance').eq('id', uid).maybeSingle()).data?.mco_balance ?? null;\n\ntry {\n  const email = `aeo-refund-${stamp}@example.com`, password = `Aa1!${crypto.randomUUID()}`;\n  const { data: cu, error } = await admin.auth.admin.createUser({ email, password, email_confirm: true });\n  if (error || !cu?.user) throw new Error(`createUser: ${error?.message}`);\n  const uid = cu.user.id; uids.push(uid);\n\n  // Deterministic starting balance.\n  await admin.from('profiles').update({ mco_balance: 20 }).eq('id', uid);\n  check('seed balance = 20', (await balanceOf(uid)) === 20, `bal=${await balanceOf(uid)}`);\n\n  // \u2500\u2500 1. Charge then refund the EXACT amount \u2192 balance restored (the aeo-audit 5-mco failure path). \u2500\u2500\n  const AEO = 5;\n  const { error: dErr } = await admin.rpc('deduct_mco_coins', { p_user_id: uid, p_amount: AEO });\n  check('deduct 5 (charge) \u2192 15', !dErr && (await balanceOf(uid)) === 15, `err=${dErr?.message} bal=${await balanceOf(uid)}`);\n  const { error: rErr } = await admin.rpc('add_mco_coins', { p_user_id: uid, p_amount: AEO });\n  check('add_mco_coins 5 (refund) \u2192 20 restored', !rErr && (await balanceOf(uid)) === 20, `err=${rErr?.message} bal=${await balanceOf(uid)}`);\n\n  // \u2500\u2500 2. add_mco_coins guard: a non-positive refund is rejected (can never mint silently). \u2500\u2500\n  const { error: negErr } = await admin.rpc('add_mco_coins', { p_user_id: uid, p_amount: -10 });\n  check('add_mco_coins -10 \u2192 rejected (guard)', !!negErr, `err=${negErr?.message ?? 'none'}`);\n  check('balance unchanged after rejected refund (still 20)', (await balanceOf(uid)) === 20, `bal=${await balanceOf(uid)}`);\n\n  // \u2500\u2500 3. lead-score 1-mco round-trip leaves balance identical (net-zero on charge+refund). \u2500\u2500\n  await admin.rpc('deduct_mco_coins', { p_user_id: uid, p_amount: 1 });\n  await admin.rpc('add_mco_coins', { p_user_id: uid, p_amount: 1 });\n  check('lead-score 1-mco charge+refund net-zero \u2192 20', (await balanceOf(uid)) === 20, `bal=${await balanceOf(uid)}`);\n} catch (e) {\n  pass = false; console.error('\u274c smoke error:', e instanceof Error ? e.message : e);\n} finally {\n  for (const id of uids) await admin.from('mcorch_nodes').delete().eq('user_id', id);\n  for (const id of uids) await admin.auth.admin.deleteUser(id);\n  console.log(`\\n\ud83e\uddf9 cleanup: ${uids.length} throwaway user(s) + mesh nodes deleted`);\n  console.log(pass ? '\\n\u2705\u2705 AEO-REFUND PRIMITIVE SMOKE PASSED' : '\\n\u274c AEO-REFUND SMOKE FAILED');\n  process.exit(pass ? 0 : 1);\n}\n"
}
```

Maestro, fechei o próximo passo seguro de maior valor: **OTD-AEO-REFUND**. O `aeo-audit` cobrava 5 mcoCoins na entrada e, se a persistência falhasse, devolvia erro **sem estornar** — o tenant perdia saldo por nada. Agora há o helper compartilhado `refundMco` (via `add_mco_coins`, simétrico ao débito, nunca lança), e tanto `aeo-audit` quanto `lead-score` estornam em qualquer falha pós-cobrança. Provei o primitivo **materialmente ao vivo** (saldo 20→15→20).

**Decisão de disciplina (probe-first):** dos três próximos passos, **só este era construível sem você**. Os outros dois estão genuinamente bloqueados em decisões suas:
- **Nurturing externo** → precisa de um **domínio verificado no Resend** (não há nada de Resend no código ainda — é setup de infra/DNS seu).
- **1º disparo pago E2E** → precisa do seu **GO econômico** (vai gastar mcoCoins reais e publicar de verdade).

Não construí o que dependia de você para não fabricar progresso. Quando você verificar o domínio Resend **ou** der o GO do primeiro run pago, eu sigo na hora. Qual dos dois você quer destravar primeiro — ou prefere que eu ataque o **OTD-INTENT-DISPATCH-REFUND** (refund no `orchestrate-content`, dentro do flywheel) enquanto isso?
